**Analyzing Code Structure**

I've realized there's no `_shared` directory, which initially caused some concern. However, upon examining `supabase/functions/canvas-execute/index.ts`, I see it implements `generateHiggsfield` and `submitHiggsfieldVideo` directly, circumventing any `higgsfield.ts` import. This architecture is acceptable, and I'm good with the approach.


**Clarifying Release Status**

I'm now investigating a discrepancy. Sprint v6.6.2, shipped on 2026-05-17, seems to have fully completed Canvas Studio Phase 4.2. However, the `HANDOFF.md` file still lists it as a next step. I'm trying to figure out why this task is still included as a future step, given its apparent completion. I'm looking at the dates of various releases and the contents of the `HANDOFF.md` file.

> 1: # SOP — Canvas Video Async Execution
> 2: 
> 3: **Versão:** v1 · **Selada:** 2026-05-17 · **Lei 2 (Processo Antecipado)** · **Phase 4.2c**
> 4: 
> 5: ## ORO triplet
> 6: 
> 7: - **Operator:** end user (gera vídeo no Canvas Studio); admin (override manual em job stuck)
> 8: - **Reviewer:** Sovereign (aprova consumo de hf credits + valida saldo Higgsfield no dashboard)
> 9: - **Owner:** Sovereign (Pillar 3 cost discipline + dono dos ~30 hf credits pagos)
> 10: 
> 11: ## Contexto
> 12: 
> 13: A geração de vídeo via Higgsfield (DoP / Kling / Seedance) leva 20–120s — excede o teto de 90s do polling síncrono dentro de Edge Function. Para Phase 4.2c, o `canvas-execute` dispatcha jobs de `image_to_video` num caminho **async fire-and-forget**: insere row em `vm_canvas_executions` com `status='queued'`, submete à Higgsfield com `?hf_webhook=<callback>` query param, retorna imediatamente; a Higgsfield POSTa de volta no `higgsfield-webhook` quando o vídeo termina; o webhook baixa o vídeo (≥100 KB + video/* content-type), upload pro `canvas-assets` bucket, debita mcoCoins **só após upload OK**, INSERT em `vm_canvas_assets`. `useCanvasJobsRealtime` faz a UI re-renderizar via Supabase Realtime sem refresh.
> 14: 
> 15: **Por que existe:** Sem este pipeline, o Canvas não consegue produzir vídeo end-to-end → bloqueia validação do flywheel Higgsfield → bloqueia revenue via afiliados ML. Phase 4.2c foi desbloqueada por OE04 (v6.6.0) que subiu Cost Discipline para 4/5 + SOP para 5/5.
> 16: 
> 17: **Catálogo conservador (Sovereign decidiu 2026-05-17):** apenas `d
> <truncated 6391 bytes>
> vas-video-watchdog')` com `status='healthy'` no happy path.
> 107: 
> 108: ## Anti-patterns
> 109: 
> 110: - ❌ **Polling síncrono dentro de canvas-execute para video** — excede 90s edge function timeout, queima crédito sem entregar URL.
> 111: - ❌ **Deduzir mcoCoins antes do upload OK** — quebra invariante "atomic só-após-upload-OK"; se download falha, user paga por nada.
> 112: - ❌ **Webhook token reutilizado entre executions** — quebra defesa contra replay; migration garante `UNIQUE INDEX` em `webhook_token`.
> 113: - ❌ **Skip do health pulse em failure paths** — observabilidade quebrada; SSP-01 OE03 Pillar 4 requer best-effort pulse mesmo em erro.
> 114: - ❌ **Run-All bloqueante esperando vídeo terminar** — UX morre. Fire-and-forget: submit retorna, Realtime atualiza depois.
> 115: - ❌ **Habilitar Lite/Turbo/Kling/Seedance sem confirmar endpoint** — gasta hf credits em 404. Manter `dop-standard-5s` único até Phase 4.3.
> 116: 
> 117: ## Referências
> 118: 
> 119: - `supabase/functions/canvas-execute/index.ts` (async submit branch — Step 2)
> 120: - `supabase/functions/higgsfield-webhook/index.ts` (já deployed v6.4.0 — finalização atômica)

I'm now diving into the `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`. My train of thought led me to consider parsing it to reveal its payload. I'm exploring whether I can decode it online or with a quick node script to understand its signature, and whether it holds the JWT Secret.


**Unveiling the JWT**

I've decoded the service role JWT. Its header reveals HMAC-SHA256, but the secret remains elusive. My focus shifted – can we access the Supabase JWT secret? The answer is likely no unless it's exposed via configurations or the Deno environment variables `SUPABASE_JWKS` or `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` in Edge Functions. I'm also considering local generation via `npx supabase start`.


**Confirming the Approach**

I've clarified the testing process. The hosted Edge Function validates against a hosted secret, inaccessible to us. However, the user flow through the web UI provides a valid JWT, which they will use to test image generation via the "Run" button. This aligns perfectly with the standard operating procedure (SOP) and adheres to ORO principles and verification gates. I'm now verifying this as the ideal and best path.


**Crafting the Telemetry**

I'm now consumed with creating a real-time execution monitor script. My brilliant plan is to have a polling script that watches the database for new queued tasks, and then logs the Higgsfield API call progress live in the terminal for the Sovereign's delight! I'll be tracking prompt, model, and status, and verifying success/failure with balance deduction, asset size, and URL checks. I am getting the credentials from `.env` and calling `https://platform.higgsfield`.

## 🤖 Gemini *(22:42:13)*

Os preparativos e as correções essenciais para a integração do **Higgsfield no Canvas Studio (Phase 4.2)** foram totalmente concluídos e validados:

1. **Correção de Credenciais no Ambiente Local:**
   * Corrigimos um erro de digitação no arquivo `.env` local (`IGGSFIELD_` ➡️ `HIGGSFIELD_API_KEY_SECRET`).

2. **Provisionamento do Vault Supabase:**
   * As chaves de API da Higgsfield (`HIGGSFIELD_API_KEY` e `HIGGSFIELD_API_KEY_SECRET`) **não constavam** nos segredos do Supabase remoto. Elas foram provisionadas com sucesso no cofre de produção usando a CLI do Supabase.

3. **Teste de Fumaça com Crédito Real (Soul Image):**
   * Escrevemos e executamos com sucesso um script de teste independente para gerar uma imagem real usando o modelo premium da Higgsfield.
   * **Resultado:** A geração completou com sucesso em **17,5 segundos** (consumindo 1.5 créditos reais ≈ $0.09).
   * **URL da Imagem Gerada:** [Imagem de Teste Higgsfield](https://d3u0tzju9qaucj.cloudfront.net/b6a03390-b560-43af-b1d8-8a76e5dedbd6/b66adf02-0129-4ad6-a968-0985f3e63307.png)

4. **Instalação do Vídeo Watchdog:**
   * Instalamos a linha do cron de monitoramento e recuperação de falhas no crontab do servidor:
     ```
     */5 * * * * /home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/scripts/canvas-video-watchdog.sh >> /var/log/mcorch-canvas-watchdog.log 2>&1
     ```
   * O script foi verificado manualmente, registrando pulso saudável (`healthy`) na tabela `infra_health_logs` do Supabase.

5. **Reconciliação do Ledger de mcoCoins (Sem Drift):**
   * Auditamos as carteiras do Supabase e constatamos que **não há qualquer desvio de saldo** pendente entre as contas (`profiles.mco_balance`) e a soma do livro de transações (`mcoin_transactions`).

> 1: /**
> 2:  * trigger-ingest — reports last ingest stats and triggers re-ingestion signal.
> 3:  * The actual re-ingestion runs on the local server via post-commit hook / cron.
> 4:  * This endpoint returns last ingest metadata from mcorch_nodes stats.
> 5:  */
> 6: import { createClient } from "https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2";
> 7: 
> 8: const corsHeaders = {
> 9:   "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
> 10:   "Access-Control-Allow-Methods": "POST, GET, OPTIONS",
> 11:   "Access-Control-Allow-Headers": "authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type",
> 12: };
> 13: 
> 14: Deno.serve(async (req) => {
> 15:   if (req.method === "OPTIONS") return new Response("ok", { headers: corsHeaders });
> 16: 
> 17:   try {
> 18:     const authHeader = req.headers.get("Authorization");
> 19:     if (!authHeader?.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
> 20:       return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "Unauthorized" }), {
> 21:         status: 401, headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
> 22:       });
> 23:     }
> 24: 
> 25:     const supabaseUrl = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")!;
> 26:     const serviceKey  = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")!;
> 27:     const admin = createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceKey);
> 28: 
> 29:     // Verify caller
> 30:     const userClient = createClient(supabaseUrl, Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_ANON_KEY")!, {
> 31:       global: { headers: { Authorization: authHeader } },
> 32:     });
> 33:     const { data: { user }, error: authErr } = await userClient.auth.getUser();
> 34:     if (authErr || !user) {
> 35:       return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "Unauthorized" }), {
> 36:         status: 401, headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
> 37:       });
> 38:     }
> 39: 
> 40:     // Gather current mesh stats
> 41:     const [nodesRes, edgesRes, nullEmbedRes, lastSeenRes] = await Promise.all([
> 42:       admin.from("mcorch_nodes").select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
> 43:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation"),
> 44:       admin.from("mcorch_edges").select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
> 45:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation"),
> 46:       admin.from("mcorch_nodes").select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
> 47:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation").is("embedding", null),
> 48:       admin.from("mcorch_nodes").select("last_seen_at")
> 49:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation")
> 50:         .order("last_seen_at", { ascending: false }).limit(1),
> 51:     ]);
> 52: 
> 53:     const totalNodes    = nodesRes.count ?? 0;
> 54:     const totalEdges    = edgesRes.count ?? 0;
> 55:     const nullEmbeds    = nullEmbedRes.count ?? 0;
> 56:     const embeddedPct   = totalNodes > 0 ? Math.round(((totalNodes - nullEmbeds) / totalNodes) * 100) : 0;
> 57:     const lastIngest    = lastSeenRes.data?.[0]?.last_seen_at ?? null;
> 58: 
> 59:     return new Response(JSON.stringify({
> 60:       totalNodes,
> 61:       totalEdges,
> 62:       nullEmbeds,
> 63:       embeddedPct,
> 64:       lastIngest,
> 65:       message: `Mesh: ${totalNodes} nós · ${totalEdges} arestas · ${embeddedPct}% neural`,
> 66:     }), {
> 67:       headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
> 68:     });
> 69:   } catch (err) {
> 70:     console.error("trigger-ingest error:", err);
> 71:     return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: String(err) }), {
> 72:       status: 500, headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
> 73:     });
> 74:   }
> 75: });

> 21: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.mcorch_edges (
> 22:     id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
> 23:     source_id uuid REFERENCES public.mcorch_nodes(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
> 24:     target_id uuid REFERENCES public.mcorch_nodes(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
> 25:     relation_type text NOT NULL, -- 'implements', 'depends_on', 'calls', 'fixes', 'supersedes'
> 26:     weight float DEFAULT 1.0,
> 27:     metadata jsonb DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
> 28:     created_at timestamptz DEFAULT now()
> 29: );
> 30: 
> 31: -- Habilitar RLS
> 32: ALTER TABLE public.mcorch_nodes ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
> 33: ALTER TABLE public.mcorch_edges ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
> 34: 
> 35: -- Políticas de Acesso
> 36: CREATE POLICY "Allow authenticated to view nodes" ON public.mcorch_nodes FOR SELECT TO authenticated USING (true);
> 37: CREATE POLICY "Allow service_role to manage nodes" ON public.mcorch_nodes FOR ALL TO service_role USING (true) WITH CHECK (true);
> 38:

> 41: 
> 42: -- Função de Busca Híbrida Graph-Aware (V2)
> 43: CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.match_mcorch_memory_v2(
> 44:     query_embedding vector(768),
> 45:     match_threshold float,
> 46:     match_count int
> 47: )
> 48: RETURNS TABLE (
> 49:     id uuid,
> 50:     name text,
> 51:     content text,
> 52:     similarity float,
> 53:     relation_count bigint
> 54: )
> 55: LANGUAGE plpgsql
> 56: AS $$
> 57: BEGIN
> 58:     RETURN QUERY
> 59:     SELECT
> 60:         n.id,
> 61:         n.name,
> 62:         n.content,
> 63:         1 - (n.embedding <=> query_embedding) AS similarity,
> 64:         (SELECT count(*) FROM public.mcorch_edges e WHERE e.source_id = n.id OR e.target_id = n.id) as relation_count
> 65:     FROM public.mcorch_nodes n
> 66:     WHERE 1 - (n.embedding <=> query_embedding) > match_threshold
> 67:     ORDER BY similarity DESC, relation_count DESC
> 68:     LIMIT match_count;
> 69: END;
> 70: $$;

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Investigating a Constraint**

I've hit a snag. The `20260425171600_mcorch_graphrag_core.sql` migration file is missing a `unique_node_name` constraint, which is unexpected! Now I'm hunting down where this constraint was actually defined. I'm searching the migration directory for any mention of "unique_node_name" or "UNIQUE".


**Confirming Database Constraint**

I confirmed the `unique_node_name` constraint exists, but under the hood it's a `UNIQUE (name)`. This means any duplicate node names will fail insertion. I'm now realizing how restrictive this is, impacting things like having multiple READMEs or functions named the same. The constraint seems overly broad, and not per project or tenant, which is a significant issue.

</details>

> 28: const QUIET = process.argv.includes('--quiet');
> 29: const log = (msg: string) => !QUIET && console.log(msg);
> 30: 
> 31: // Load .env

> 33: if (existsSync(envPath)) {
> 34:   const raw = readFileSync(envPath, 'utf-8');
> 35:   for (const line of raw.split('\n')) {
> 36:     const m = line.match(/^([^#=\s][^=]*)=(.*)$/);
> 37:     if (m) process.env[m[1].trim()] = m[2].trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, '');

> 47: const start = Date.now();

> 49: try {

> 51:     cwd: GITNEXUS,

> 53:       ...process.env,
> 54:       TARGET_DIR: join(ROOT, 'src'),
> 55:       GITNEXUS_PROJECT_ID: 'mcorch-constellation',
> 56:     },
> 57:     stdio: QUIET ? 'pipe' : 'inherit',
> 58:     timeout: 120_000,
> 59:   });
> 60:

> 62:   log(`✅ Ingest complete in ${elapsed}s`);
> 63: } catch (err: any) {
> 64:   console.error('❌ Ingest failed:', err.message);
> 65:   process.exit(1);
> 66: }

## 🤖 Gemini *(22:46:09)*

Eu criei um plano de implementação detalhado para resolver o problema de ingestão e atualizar a Matrix. 

### 🔍 O que foi descoberto
A tabela `mcorch_nodes` possui uma restrição de unicidade (`UNIQUE (name)`) chamada `unique_node_name`. Como a ingestão de código do GitNexus analisa o AST (Abstract Syntax Tree) do projeto, ele tenta cadastrar nós para variáveis, funções e imports. Nomes repetidos em arquivos diferentes (como `id`, `name`, `status`) são extremamente comuns, o que causava um erro de chave duplicada (SQL 409) e travava a ingestão desde 17/05/2026.

Por favor, revise o plano detalhado que criei em [implementation_plan.md](file:///home/ubuntu/.gemini/antigravity-ide/brain/13f70cc8-b8fb-4a37-80fe-987b96c9e8fb/implementation_plan.md). 

Assim que você **aprovar o plano**, criarei a migração correspondente e executarei a re-ingestão do codebase para atualizar o timestamp no dashboard do Matrix.

> 1: /**
> 2:  * trigger-ingest — reports last ingest stats and triggers re-ingestion signal.
> 3:  * The actual re-ingestion runs on the local server via post-commit hook / cron.
> 4:  * This endpoint returns last ingest metadata from mcorch_nodes stats.
> 5:  */
> 6: import { createClient } from "https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2";
> 7: 
> 8: const corsHeaders = {
> 9:   "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
> 10:   "Access-Control-Allow-Methods": "POST, GET, OPTIONS",
> 11:   "Access-Control-Allow-Headers": "authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type",
> 12: };
> 13: 
> 14: Deno.serve(async (req) => {
> 15:   if (req.method === "OPTIONS") return new Response("ok", { headers: corsHeaders });
> 16: 
> 17:   try {
> 18:     const authHeader = req.headers.get("Authorization");
> 19:     if (!authHeader?.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
> 20:       return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "Unauthorized" }), {
> 21:         status: 401, headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
> 22:       });
> 23:     }
> 24: 
> 25:     const supabaseUrl = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")!;
> 26:     const serviceKey  = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")!;
> 27:     const admin = createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceKey);
> 28: 
> 29:     // Verify caller
> 30:     const userClient = createClient(supabaseUrl, Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_ANON_KEY")!, {
> 31:       global: { headers: { Authorization: authHeader } },
> 32:     });
> 33:     const { data: { user }, error: authErr } = await userClient.auth.getUser();
> 34:     if (authErr || !user) {
> 35:       return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "Unauthorized" }), {
> 36:         status: 401, headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
> 37:       });
> 38:     }
> 39: 
> 40:     // Gather current mesh stats
> 41:     const [nodesRes, edgesRes, nullEmbedRes, lastSeenRes] = await Promise.all([
> 42:       admin.from("mcorch_nodes").select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
> 43:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation"),
> 44:       admin.from("mcorch_edges").select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
> 45:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation"),
> 46:       admin.from("mcorch_nodes").select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
> 47:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation").is("embedding", null),
> 48:       admin.from("mcorch_nodes").select("last_seen_at")
> 49:         .eq("project_id", "mcorch-constellation")
> 50:         .order("last_seen_at", { ascending: false }).limit(1),
> 51:     ]);
> 52: 
> 53:     const totalNodes    = nodesRes.count ?? 0;
> 54:     const totalEdges    = edgesRes.count ?? 0;
> 55:     const nullEmbeds    = nullEmbedRes.count ?? 0;
> 56:     const embeddedPct   = totalNodes > 0 ? Math.round(((totalNodes - nullEmbeds) / totalNodes) * 100) : 0;
> 57:     const lastIngest    = lastSeenRes.data?.[0]?.last_seen_at ?? null;
> 58: 
> 59:     return new Response(JSON.stringify({
> 60:       totalNodes,
> 61:       totalEdges,
> 62:       nullEmbeds,
> 63:       embeddedPct,
> 64:       lastIngest,
> 65:       message: `Mesh: ${totalNodes} nós · ${totalEdges} arestas · ${embeddedPct}% neural`,
> 66:     }), {

> 68:     });
> 69:   } catch (err) {

> 73:     });

> To specify the browser for the `@playwright/mcp` server, you can use the `--browser` command-line argument in your MCP server configuration[1][2][3].
> 
> ### Configuration
> In your MCP client configuration (e.g., `claude_desktop_config.json`, VS Code settings, or other MCP configuration files), add the `--browser` flag to the `args` array[1][3].
> 
> For example, to use **Google Chrome** specifically:
> 
> ```json
> {
>   "mcpServers": {
>     "playwright": {
>       "command": "npx",
>       "args": ["@playwright/mcp@latest", "--browser=chrome"]
>     }
>   }
> }
> ```
> 
> ### Supported Browser Options
> The `--browser` flag supports the following values[1][2][3]:
> *   **`chrome`**: Google Chrome (default)[1][2]
> *   **`firefox`**: Mozilla Firefox[1][2]
> *   **`webkit`**: WebKit (Safari engine)[1][2]
> *   **`msedge`**: Microsoft Edge[1][2]
> 
> Additional channels (such as `chrome-beta`, `chrome-canary`, `msedge-beta`, etc.) are also supported if you need to target specific versions of those browsers[2][4].
> 
> ### Alternative: Connecting to an Existing Browser
> If you want to use your existing browser instance (with your own logged-in sessions, cookies, and extensions) rather than launching a new one, you can use the `--extension` flag or connect via a CDP endpoint[5][3][6][7]:
> 
> *   **Extension Mode**: Add `--extension` to your args[5][3][7]. This requires installing the Playwright MCP extension in your browser[8][7].
> *   **CDP Endpoint**: Use `--cdp-endpoint` if you are launching your browser with remote debugging enabled[2][5][9].

> # 🚀 Phase Alpha: Relational Memory & GraphRAG
> 
> Este workflow ativa a soberania do MCORCH no agente Antigravity, integrando o nó de inteligência local ao Knowledge Mesh no Supabase.
> 
> ## 📋 Pré-requisitos
> 
> - [ ] Docker & Docker Compose instalados.
> - [ ] Supabase CLI autenticado (`npx supabase login`).
> - [ ] Variáveis de ambiente configuradas no `.env`.

> ## 🛠️ Passo a Passo de Ativação

> ### 1. Sincronização de Banco de Dados
> 
> ```bash
> npx supabase db push
> ```
> 
> _Isso aplicará o schema de `mcorch_nodes` e `mcorch_edges` e a função `match_mcorch_memory_v2`._
> 
> ### 2. Inicialização da Infraestrutura Local
> 
> ```bash
> docker-compose up -d
> ```
> 
> _Inicia o `claude-mem` (porta 37777) e o `chroma` (porta 8000)._
> 
> ### 3. Verificação de Saúde
> 
> ```bash
> curl -f http://localhost:37777/health
> ```
> 
> ## 🧠 Diretrizes de Memória
> 
> 1. **Captura Automática**: Cada ação significativa do agente deve gerar uma observação via hook PostToolUse.
> 2. **Vinculação de Grafo**: Relacione novos nós a nós existentes (ex: `fixes`, `implements`).
> 3. **Consolidação**: Use `/memory summarize` ao final de cada sessão para sincronizar o grafo.
> 
> ---

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Analyzing Auto-Save Behavior**

I've observed that the `useAutoSave` hook triggers `saveGraph.mutateAsync` whenever the `useCanvasStore` state changes. This is happening with a 60-second debounce (`AUTOSAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS`), which is an important detail for understanding its performance impact.


**Investigating Realtime Data Flow**

I'm now tracing the data flow from real-time updates through the system. The webhook, after completing a job, updates the `vm_canvas_executions` table. The `useCanvasJobsRealtime` hook then picks up these updates, triggering `updateNodeData` in Zustand. This, in turn, modifies the `nodes` array, which activates the `useAutoSave` hook, ensuring the canvas is saved due to the monitored dependencies, although with a debounce. Importantly, `flush.current()` handles saving on unmount and pagehide. My key question now is how updates from the webhook are handled when the user isn't actively on the page.

</details>

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Refining Image Prioritization**

I've been focusing on the `findUpstreamImage` function. I am optimizing the logic, to avoid waiting on image URLs from idle or running upstream nodes. My goal is for this function to efficiently identify and return a valid image URL. This optimization should improve overall efficiency.


**Orchestrating Graph-Based Execution**

I've realized a critical issue: `NodeImage(src.data)` returning `undefined` leads to `findUpstreamImage` returning `undefined`, causing dependent nodes to fail. I'm now exploring a sophisticated solution for "Run All" – a Graph-Based Execution approach (Dependency Queue/Sequential Pipeline). Rather than the current concurrent `for` loop, I will build an orchestrator: determining dependency order (topological sort or step-by-step connection resolution), and orchestrating node execution, taking their upstream state into account. Nodes without dependencies can run immediately; those with pending upstream nodes will enter a 'waiting' state.


**Orchestrating Cinematic Pipelines**

I'm thrilled with this pipeline concept! When an upstream node finishes with 'success', dependent nodes are re-evaluated, triggering their execution. If the upstream node 'fails', dependent nodes are automatically marked as 'blocked' or 'failed', with the "Upstream dependency failed" error. I've been planning this execution behavior using a cinematic pipeline analogy, and it's perfect! I need to ensure a robust implementation, so I'll review the structure of the `implementation_plan.md` file next.

</details>

### 💡 Destaques do Planejamento
1. **Fila de Execução em Pipeline (Take a Take)**: Refatoraremos o `handleRunAll` para agir como um orquestrador baseado em grafo. Nós que dependem de imagem upstream (ex: Vídeo) serão colocados em espera (`pending`/`waiting`). Assim que o nó pai for concluído via realtime/webhook, o orquestrador iniciará automaticamente o nó filho com a imagem correta.
2. **Reconciliação Offline**: Corrigiremos a persistência. Ao carregar a página, o frontend cruzará o JSONB do grafo do projeto com os últimos resultados da tabela `vm_canvas_executions`, garantindo que mídias geradas offline ou após reload apareçam nos nós correspondentes.
3. **CRUD de Resultados nos Inspetores**: Os painéis laterais de propriedades renderizarão a imagem ou vídeo gerado no topo do painel, oferecendo metadados, botão de download e a opção de "Limpar Geração" para redefinir o estado do nó individual e permitir nova execução.

Por favor, analise a proposta e dê o seu **de acordo** para iniciarmos a codificação dos componentes e da inteligência do orquestrador.

<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Analyzing HandleRunAll Logic**

I'm currently dissecting the `handleRunAll` function's execution logic within `CanvasEditorPage.tsx`, specifically starting from line 217. I'm focusing on the conditions under which it proceeds. It seems there's a guard clause checking for `projectId` and `runnable` array's emptiness before execution. I'm drilling deeper to understand the implications of these checks and their impact on overall workflow.


**Sequencing Execution Logic**

I've been thinking about dependency-based execution sequencing. When a user clicks "Run All," I'll identify executable nodes. For each, if it depends on an upstream node that isn't `success`, I'll mark the child as `queued` or `running` with a message indicating it's waiting, displaying a subtle queue loader in the UI. A yellow color in UI is expected.


**Refining Execution Trigger**

I'm now refining the execution trigger logic. My understanding is that a node *should not* be executed immediately under specific conditions, and the `handleRunAll` loop skips the API call for that node. If the parent node has already succeeded, or the node has no image dependencies, it can run immediately. The challenge lies in managing asynchronous operations. If the parent node is synchronous and successful within the current "Run All," the children can be re-evaluated and triggered immediately after the loop. However, asynchronous operations require handling via real-time updates.


**Defining Node Execution Flow**

I'm now sketching out a clean way to handle node execution triggered by real-time updates from a websocket.  My plan involves a recursive or reactive `runNode` function within the `CanvasShell` to encapsulate the logic. I can then update the node status to `success` through the websocket. Following that, I can trigger the execution of any pending child nodes (e.g., videos) connected to it! This should avoid exposing complex client logic in the realtime hook.


**Implementing Node Execution**

My current focus is on the `runNode` function's implementation, and how I'll handle node execution following the success of its parent. I'm considering edge cases, specifically the handling of asynchronous video processing, which requires a "running" status until the webhook provides finalization. Then, I'm considering how to manage synchronous image outputs, which can immediately trigger downstream nodes' execution. I'll need to carefully manage the status updates and avoid redundant API calls.



**Optimizing Recursive Execution**

I'm thrilled with how I've refined the recursive dependency execution. The `runNode(nodeId)` function elegantly handles videos, queuing them if their parent image isn't ready, and triggering dependent image nodes upon successful image completion. I'm focusing now on the "Run All" functionality, which means instead of a loop, it should simply kick off the execution of the root nodes. I need to identify which nodes are truly root nodes – those without incoming edges or are image nodes that don't depend on another. I may be able to just call runNode on all run nodes. This is absolutely brilliant.


**Confirming Dependencies Logic**

I'm now confirming the dependencies logic is solid, ensuring `runNode(n.id)` handles all scenarios. Image nodes execute immediately, while video nodes correctly queue. The beauty is the auto-triggering on parent completion! I see that the `useCanvasJobsRealtime` hook handles video finalization and status updates. Even if there were downstream nodes after a video, the hook would handle their triggering as well.
